20210209	I'm also not going to explain what options are in this video.
20210209	This is more of an I understand what options are but don't know how to make money with them yet video.
20210209	So if you're one of the 8 million members of Wall Street Bets, this might help you.
20210209	A large part of an option's price is the volatility of the underlying stock.
20210209	Which would you pay more for?
20210209	A 5% out of the money call where the underlying stock has regular daily fluctuations of 5%?
20210209	Or a 5% out of the money call where the underlying stock has regular daily fluctuations of 1%?
20210209	Assuming both options have 5 days until expiration, you'd probably pay more for the first call option.
20210209	That's because it's more likely to be in the money at some point before your expiration date.
20210209	This is very important to understand if you want to have any success trading options.
20210209	Ideally you want to buy an option when its implied volatility is very low and sell when it's much higher.
20210209	There are a lot of strategies that go along with this that I'll discuss in the future.
20210209	However for now, before you drop your life savings into an option play, take a look at the historic implied volatility of the stock.
20210209	Then look at the implied volatility right now and see if you're buying an option at relatively high level.
20210209	Keep in mind that you're trying to buy options with low IV relative to their historic average IV.
20210209	Just because Tesla looks like a heart rate monitor doesn't mean there isn't an opportunity there.
20210209	As long as IV goes up relative to what it is now, you're in the green.
20210209	When you buy shares of stock, the only thing you have to predict is what direction the stock will go, and that's usually pretty easy because stocks only go up.
20210209	However, with options you not only have to predict the direction the stock will move, but but also how much it will move and how quickly.
20210209	This is a massive reason why people lose money trading options.
20210209	You can be right with your prediction, but are you right enough, and are you right more than what was anticipated by the market?
20210209	When other people predict that a stock will be volatile, option prices go up, and degenerates on Wall Street bets buy them because they think the stock is about to make a big move.
20210209	The problem is that everyone thinks the stock is about to make a big move, meaning that the options are priced efficiently for a big move.
20210209	When it inevitably doesn't make as big a move as anticipated, IV drops and degenerates lose their student loans.
20210209	Understanding the various ways implied volatility will either fuck your position or make you money is one of the most important things to learn about options early on.
20210209	IV can legitimately destroy your entire trade if you aren't careful.
20210209	It can also make you a lot of money if you play it right.
20210209	If you're planning to put any significant amount of money on an option play, I'd recommend buying at least a month until expiration.
20210209	These YOLOs with just days until expiration are insanely retarded and you're very likely to lose money on them.
20210209	Because options have expiration dates, they decrease in value every day that they're out of the money.
20210209	This value is called theta, and it's one of the long lists of complicated stonk things that'll steal your fortune faster than Mackenzie Scott.
20210209	A call option with a current price of $2 and a theta of negative .05 will experience a drop in price of 5 cents per day.
20210209	So in two days time, the price of the option will fall to $1.90.
20210209	However, what happens when you buy an option with three days until expiration?
20210209	The contract itself may only be worth 15 cents in total.
20210209	That means that the same theta of .05 is burning 30% of your initial investment per day.
20210209	This is how theta can ruin your life.
20210209	Another issue with buying options that are close to expiration is that you're at the complete mercy of the randomness and impulsiveness of the stock market.
20210209	Jeff Bezos could tweet tomorrow that he's going to be moving his primary residence to Little St.
20210209	All of a sudden your calls are out of the money, not to say that options with a month until expiration make you completely invulnerable to this.
20210209	However, if you've bought longer term options, your position will at least be manageable when Tim Cook claims that he's going to be yoloing most of his Apple share into Palantir F.D.'s.
20210209	And it will happen, like Johnny Cash said.
20210209	You can run on for a long time, sooner or later Elon Musk will tweet that the stock is too high while you're holding calls.
20210209	Not understanding the risks of implied volatility and the risks of short term options are two of the biggest things that have caused me to lose money in the past.
20210209	Like we can see in the retard differential.
20210209	When you start trading options, it's really easy to think that puts in calls or a coin flip.
20210209	Just keep in mind that there are other factors at play, for better and for worse.

20210301	With a covered call, you're buying 100 shares of stock, then selling a call against those 100 shares.
20210301	This means that if you want to make a lot of premium, you need to own a huge amount of shares.
20210301	And because I'd always recommend selling covered calls against blue chip stocks, it requires a lot of money that you don't have.

20210301	So instead, what I'll do is buy an in the money call on the same stock with 3 to 12 months until expiration.
20210301	In general, you want to look for a call that has around a 70 delta, or a 70% chance of being in the money at expiration.
20210301	This call also functions like roughly 70 shares of stock.
20210301	But what I can do now is sell a call against the call we just bought with 30 to 45 days until expiration and a 30 delta, meaning this call has a 30% chance of being in the money at expiration.
20210301	Now I'm collecting the same premium I would be if I were holding 100 shares, but it cost me a fraction of the price.
20210301	Obviously, this does come with extra risk.

20210301	Our long call also provides us with more leverage than if we were just holding shares, which can be a good thing or a very bad thing.
20210301	Here are a few incredibly important things to keep in mind when using this strategy.
20210301	Obviously, taking on more risk than if you were to just own shares.
20210301	This risk manifests itself in a few different ways.
20210301	The fact that your option has time until it expires gives it extrinsic value.
20210301	In a practical sense, this means that the underlying will have to go up throughout the life of the option in order for you to break even on your long call.
20210301	If we look here on optionprofitcalculator.com, we can see that our break even share price increases as we get closer to expiration.
20210301	Like any option play, you not only have to be right your prediction, but you have to be right before a set date and right enough.
20210301	It also means that the basis of this strategy should always be buying a call that will increase in value, not hunting high premium.
20210301	Collecting premium is a way to provide downside protection and lower the cost basis of that long call.
20210301	It's so incredibly important to make sure you buy into a good long call position and not just the one where you can collect the most premium.

20210301	That means that we can anticipate Apple's IV will most likely increase back up to that average, increasing the value of our long call.

20210301	Rolling up is just closing your short call and selling a new short call with a higher Rolling down is closing your short call and selling a new short call with a lower strike and rolling out is closing your short call and selling a new call that has a later expiration date.
20210301	There's not a specific rule that dictates when you should roll your position but this is the general idea.

20210704	However what might even be more important is how they determine which underlying stock to sell these straddles on.

20210704	Essentially, they've made a ton of money selling retail degenerates overpriced options.
20210704	You might be able to do the same.

20210704	They're looking at the spread between the stocks implied volatility and the sector's implied volatility, as well as the implied volatility of this stock relative to its historic realized volatility.
20210704	I think it's easier to imagine this visually.

20210704	More in line with sector volatility.
20210704	In this report, they say specifically that the volatility risk premium hasn't expanded uniformly across stocks with high retail option volume.

20210704	Sometimes this leads to expensive options that are disconnected from reality.
20210704	The other strategy they describe is buying long call spreads on stocks where implied volatility and realized volatility have converged.
20210704	This would be buying a long call, then selling a deeper out of the money call on a stock where this volatility risk premium is tighter.
20210704	This strategy is less volatility based, so if you're using it, you want to make sure you're buying spreads on a company you actually believe in.
20210704	Selling straddles is purely a volatility based options strategy, but with a long call spread, your volatility exposure is not nearly as large as your directional exposure.

20210929	I legitimately could not find one back test to suggest that any of these indicators are able to outperform over the long term.

20210929	Reading candlesticks, support, resistance, volume, and momentum.

20210929	Keywords there being can and periods.
20210929	This is definitely what makes technical-based strategies very difficult to implement successfully.
20210929	These strategies work until they don't, and there's little information about what causes them to work.
20210929	Let's take for example this backtest of a few trading strategies that all use the relative strength index.
20210929	The RSI measures the magnitude of recent price changes to determine whether a stock might be overbought or oversold.
20210929	It's a scale between 1 and 100, anything below 30 generally being considered oversold, anything above 70 considered overbought.
20210929	This This backtest looked at four different RSI-based strategies and how they performed against holding the underlying.
20210929	One of the four strategies actually beat not only the underlying stock but also the market between 2014 and 2021.
20210929	Past performance is no indicator of future results, but this is an example that some strategies based purely on technical indicators can work over extended periods of time.
20210929	Not only in generating a higher return, but also a higher risk-adjusted return.
20210929	Not quite the Bernie Madoff return, but we're getting there.
20210929	However, unfortunately, it's relatively easy to find a strategy that's worked for certain periods in the past.
20210929	What's difficult is recognizing when an indicator will work.
20210929	What's the market condition that's causing the indicator to predict the market accurately?
20210929	Can you figure out why this RSI Momentum strategy was working during this 8 year window?
20210929	If you can't, then how will you remain confident in the strategy during periods of underperformance?

20210929	Enough people using the same indicator actually causing the price action that you're seeing.

20210929	So should you incorporate technical analysis into your trading strategy?
20210929	For most people, I think using technical analysis as a standalone strategy will not work very well.

20210929	What seems smarter is to use some of the basic ideas of technical analysis in tandem with fundamental analysis or some other strategy.

20220502	Understanding implied volatility is critical, even if you're just using options to make directional bets on the share price.
20220502	People bought puts when GameStop was at $400 and still lost money.
20220502	Leave it to WallStreetBets to somehow fuck that up.
20220502	Volatility is defined as the annualized standard deviation of log returns.
20220502	I wouldn't be teaching you math unless it was absolutely necessary.
20220502	So what do we actually mean when we we say GameStop has a 30-day implied volatility of 400.
20220502	To understand it more simply, you can take your implied volatility and divide it by the square root of the number of trading days in the year, which conveniently turns out to be 16.
20220502	Take whatever number is on this line and divide it by 16.
20220502	This gives you the market's estimate of how much a stock is going to move each day within the lifespan of the option.
20220502	In GameStop's case, the 30-day implied volatility was suggesting the stock was going to move an average 25% per day for the next 30 days.
20220502	Which, when we say it's the estimate, we have to remember that implied volatility is implied by option prices.
20220502	And option prices are determined based on supply and demand.
20220502	The Black-Scholes formula requires inputs, one of which being an option's price.
20220502	I know, fucking complicated, but it's critical to understand.
20220502	Because if a ton of people want calls, maybe a ton of retail traders looking to squeeze hedge funds, the price goes up, regardless of what a realistic forecast of volatility might be.
20220502	Consider when GameStop's 1 year IV got up to 212%.
20220502	If we divide by 16, we can see the option market was implying GameStop to move 13% per day for the next year.
20220502	GameStop was never going to move 13% per day for a year.
20220502	Shorting that was as close to free money as you could have ever gotten.
20220502	So while yes, the option market is largely efficient, there are a lot of instances when it's not.
20220502	I like looking at GameStop because it's the most exaggerated example of the things that can happen on a smaller scale every day.

20251218	At the time, traders around the country were sending their orders to the exchanges using telecom wires.
20251218	And while these routes were fast enough for most trading strategies, there were a few highly profitable trades where they posed a huge problem.
20251218	To give you an example, one trade was arbitrage between an index futures contract that trades on the CME and the underlying stocks that make up the index, which trade on the NASDAQ.
20251218	Most of the time the futures trade where they should based on the value of the stocks.
20251218	However, traders observed that every day there are a lot of times when the value of the future gets dislocated enough against the portfolio of stocks to the point where you can buy the stocks and sell the future.
20251218	Illiterate freaks buying index futures would push the price up, and traders would act as the middlemen between these markets, dispersing the excess demand in the futures market into the supply of the stock market.
20251218	The spread between the cost of the portfolio of stocks and the profit from selling the future or vice versa was a risk-free profit, an incredibly profitable and competitive trade done by the biggest trading firms in the country.
20251218	In the olden days, humans could manually do these trades.
20251218	But as the 2000s came around, automated trading systems started to dominate more and more of the markets, and doing this trade required being much faster.
20251218	This is where Dan's gargantuan genius began.
20251218	Because he had realized the speed provided by the telecom companies was far from the fastest speed that was theoretically possible.
20251218	In order to do this trade, you need to be the first to sell the futures and buy the stocks.
20251218	Or buy the futures and sell the stocks.
20251218	When a trading firm located in Chicago sees the futures go up relative to the stocks, their algorithm races to send a sell order for the futures and to buy order for the stocks.
20251218	The problem is that the currently available telecom routes from Chicago to New Jersey aren't designed for fast trading, which means the communication speed is very inconsistent.
20251218	On one day, sending this order might take 8.5 milliseconds.
20251218	This led to an incredibly infuriating situation where a trader's algorithm might be the first to get a signal that the price has diverged, but still miss the trade due to their order going through a slower route.
20251218	This meant quite possibly seeing hundreds of thousands of dollars per day of potential profit, all while being able to capture none of it.
